Requires Claude Code v2.1.199 or later.\n* `verdict`: set when a verify pass ran; absent on inline-only reviews.\n* `outcome`: set only when re-reporting after applying fixes.\n\n### [​](#artifact) Artifact\n\n**Tool name:** `Artifact`\n\n```\ntype ArtifactInput = {  action?: \"publish\" | \"list\";  file_path?: string;  favicon?: string;  limit?: number;  scope?: \"mine\" | \"shared\" | \"all\";  title?: string;  description?: string;  label?: string;  url?: string;  force?: boolean;  capabilities?: Record<string, unknown>;  contract?: \"latest\" | string; }; \n```\n\nPublishes a local `.html` or `.md` file as a hosted artifact page, or lists the user’s published artifacts. Omit `action` or pass `\"publish\"` to publish `file_path`, which is required for the publish action along with `favicon`, one or two emoji that mark the artifact in the user’s gallery. `title` names the published page in the browser tab and gallery when the HTML file has no `</code> tag. <code>url</code> targets an existing artifact to update in place instead of minting a new one.</span> <span><code>force</code> is a last-resort overwrite that discards a newer version another session published. On a conflict, the failed publish returns the newer content; Claude merges its changes onto that content, or re-reads the artifact, and publishes again. Pass <code>force</code> only when the user explicitly asks to discard that version.</span> <span>Pass <code>\"list\"</code> to enumerate the user’s published artifacts; only <code>limit</code> and <code>scope</code> may accompany it. <code>scope</code> defaults to <code>\"mine\"</code>, which lists artifacts the user owns; <code>\"shared\"</code> lists artifacts other people shared with the user, and <code>\"all\"</code> lists both.</span>\n<ul>\n<li><code>capabilities</code>: the runtime capabilities the published page uses, keyed by capability name, such as the <a href=\"/docs/en/artifacts#pull-live-data-with-mcp-connectors\">connectors the page may call</a>. The artifact service validates the declaration and rejects a publish that names a capability the account can’t use or gives one an invalid config. Pass <code>{}</code> to clear a stored declaration, and omit the field on a redeploy to keep it. Requires Agent SDK v0.3.235 or later.</li>\n<li><code>contract</code>: the runtime version the published page runs against. Omit it to keep the artifact’s current version, pass <code>\"latest\"</code> to upgrade, or pass a specific version to pin or roll back. Requires Agent SDK v0.3.235 or later.</li> </ul> <span>The types are exported, but the tool is off by default in Agent SDK sessions. Publishing also requires every condition in the <a href=\"/docs/en/artifacts#availability\">artifacts availability table</a>, which sessions authenticated with an API key don’t meet.</span>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#projects\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span>Projects</span></h3> <span><strong>Tool name:</strong> writes the claude.ai Project attached to the session. Dispatches on <code>method</code>:</span>\n<ul>\n<li><code>project_info</code>: returns project metadata and the doc list.</li>\n<li><code>project_read</code>: reads one doc by <code>path</code>.</li>\n<li><code>project_search</code>: queries the project’s knowledge base with <code>query</code>. <code>n</code> caps the hits and defaults to <code>5</code>.</li>\n<li><code>project_write</code>: creates or replaces a doc at <code>path</code> from exactly one of <code>content</code>, which carries inline text, or <code>local_path</code>, which names a file inside the working directory. <code>present_to_user: true</code> marks the written doc as the deliverable the user needs to see.</li>\n<li><code>project_delete</code>: deletes a doc by <code>path</code>.</li> </ul>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#readmcpresourcedir\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span>ReadMcpResourceDir</span></h3> <span><strong>Tool name:</strong> style=\"--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E\"></div></div> <span>Lists the direct children of a directory resource on an MCP server. Only usable against a server that has declared support for directory listing; the listing isn’t recursive. Directory listing isn’t enabled in every session: when it’s off, the call returns an empty <code>resources</code> list and the <code>error</code> field reports that directory listing isn’t enabled.</span>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#refreshmcptools\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span>RefreshMcpTools</span></h3> <span><strong>Tool name:</strong> <code>RefreshMcpTools</code></span>\n<div>\n<div>\n<div><button></button></div>\n<div><button></button><span></span></div>\n<div><button></button></div></div>\n<div style=\"position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px;font-variant-ligatures:none;height:auto;background-color:#ffffff;--shiki-dark-bg:#0B0C0E\">\n<div style=\"overflow:scroll\">\n<div style=\"min-width:fit-content\">\n<div>\n<pre style=\"background-color:#ffffff;--shiki-dark-bg:#0B0C0E;color:#1f2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4\"><code><span><span style=\"color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#569CD6\">type</span><span style=\"color:#953800;--shiki-dark:#4EC9B0\"> RefreshMcpToolsInput</span><span style=\"color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4\"> =</span><span style=\"color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4\"> {</span></span> <span><span style=\"color:#953800;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE\"> server</span><span style=\"color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4\">?:</span><span style=\"color:#0550AE;--shiki-dark:#4EC9B0\"> string</span><span style=\"color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4\">; </span><span style=\"color:#6E7781;--shiki-dark:#6A9955\">// refresh only this server; omit to refresh all connected servers</span></span> <span><span style=\"color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4\">};</span></span> </code></pre></div></div></div></div>\n<div style=\"--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E\"></div></div> <span>Re-queries the tool list of connected MCP servers and applies any changes. The types are exported, but Claude Code registers the tool only when you set <code>CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_REFRESH_MCP_TOOLS=1</code> in the <a href=\"#options\"><code>env</code> option</a>, and only in sessions with at least one MCP server. style=\"--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E\"></div></div> <span>Returns the result from the subagent. Discriminated on the <code>status</code> field: <code>\"completed\"</code> for finished tasks, <code>\"async_launched\"</code> for background tasks, and <code>\"remote_launched\"</code> for tasks Claude Code dispatched to a cloud session, where <code>sessionUrl</code> links to that session and <code>taskId</code> identifies it.</span> <span>On the <code>completed</code> variant, <code>resolvedModel</code> names the model the subagent started on, which can differ from the requested <code>model</code> input when <a href=\"/docs/en/model-config#restrict-model-selection\"><code>availableModels</code></a> or another override applies. This field requires Claude Code v2.1.174 or later. On <code>async_launched</code>, it names the model in use when the task moved to the background.</span> <span><code>modelsUsed</code> lists the models the subagent used, in order. The field is present only when a mid-run swap happened, and a model appears again when the run swapped back to it. On <code>async_launched</code>, the list covers the models used before backgrounding. Both <code>modelsUsed</code> and the backgrounding behavior of <code>resolvedModel</code> require Claude Code v2.1.212 or later.</span> <span>If Claude Code <a href=\"/docs/en/worktrees#isolate-subagents-with-worktrees\">kept the subagent’s isolated worktree</a>, <code>worktreePath</code> on the <code>completed</code> result is where to find it. <code>worktreeBranch</code> is its branch, present when Claude Code created the worktree with git.</span> <span>Claude Code fills <code>usage</code> and <code>totalTokens</code> from the subagent’s final API request, not from the whole run, so <code>usage.service_tier</code> is the service tier string the API reported on that request. When present, <code>usage.output_tokens_details.thinking_tokens</code> is the number of that request’s output tokens that were thinking tokens. The <code>output_tokens_details</code> field requires TypeScript SDK v0.3.228 or later, which bundles Claude Code v2.1.228.</span> <span><code>usage.output_tokens_details</code> matches <a href=\"#usage\"><code>Usage.output_tokens_details</code></a> in meaning, scoped to that final request, but every level of it is optional here. Guard both the object and the field, for example <code>usage.output_tokens_details?.thinking_tokens ?? 0</code>, rather than reading it directly.</span> <span>Before v2.1.207, the published type was narrower. It omitted <code>worktreePath</code>, <code>worktreeBranch</code>, <code>citations</code>, <code>toolStats.frameCount</code>, and the <code>inference_geo</code>, <code>speed</code>, and <code>iterations</code> usage fields, and it typed <code>service_tier</code> as <code>\"standard\" | \"priority\" | \"batch\"</code>. Fields the type marks optional can be absent on results recorded by earlier versions.</span>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#askuserquestion-2\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span>AskUserQuestion</span></h3> style=\"--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E\"></div></div> <span>The <code>stdout</code>, <code>stderr</code>, and <code>backgroundTaskId</code> fields carry:</span>\n<div style=\"position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px\">\n<div style=\"overflow:scroll\">\n<div style=\"min-width:fit-content\">\n<div>\n<table><thead><tr><th>Field</th><th>What it carries</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>stdout</code></td><td>The command’s stdout and stderr, merged into one interleaved stream</td></tr><tr><td><code>stderr</code></td><td>Notices the tool itself adds, such as a shell working-directory reset, not the command’s stderr</td></tr><tr><td><code>backgroundTaskId</code></td><td>Present for background commands</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div> <span><code>timedOutAfterMs</code> is the timeout in milliseconds, set when the command reached its timeout and moved to the background rather than starting there explicitly. <code>backgroundCwdHint</code> is set when the backgrounded command contained a directory-change builtin such as <code>cd</code>, <code>pushd</code>, <code>popd</code>, or <code>chdir</code>, and notes that the session working directory didn’t change. Both fields require Claude Code v2.1.210 or later.</span> <span>When a subagent running in the foreground owns a backgrounded command, Claude Code terminates the command when that subagent gives its final response. Claude Code sets <code>backgroundEndsWithFinalResponse</code> to <code>true</code> on such commands, and omits the field when the command survives the turn, as commands started by the main conversation or by background subagents do. The field requires Claude Code v2.1.227 or later.</span> <span>Claude Code sets <code>gitOperation.commit.branch</code> to the branch named in git’s commit summary line, and omits it for a commit made on a detached HEAD. The field requires Agent SDK v0.3.227 or later. Claude Code reports a <code>gh pr reopen</code> command as the <code>reopened</code> PR action, which requires Agent SDK v0.3.234 or later.</span>\n<h3>\n<div><a publish to. Claude Code reads it back when a session resumes so a later publish builds on the right version; your code doesn’t need to act on it. <code>slug</code> names the artifact, <code>ver</code> is the version the read put on record and is absent when it recorded none, and <code>seeded: false</code> marks a read whose full source didn’t reach Claude. Check <code>error</code> before treating the run as started: a script that fails its syntax check returns <code>status: \"async_launched\"</code> with <code>error</code> set, and never runs.</span>\n<div style=\"position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px\">\n<div style=\"overflow:scroll\">\n<div style=\"min-width:fit-content\">\n<div>\n<table><thead><tr><th>Field</th><th>Type</th><th>Description</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>status</code></td><td><code>\"async_launched\" | \"remote_launched\"</code></td><td>The tool accepted the invocation. <code>\"async_launched\"</code> for in-process runs, <code>\"remote_launched\"</code> for runs dispatched to a cloud session instead of running in-process</td></tr><tr><td><code>taskId</code></td><td><code>string</code></td><td>Background task identifier for the run</td></tr><tr><td><code>taskType</code></td><td><code>\"local_workflow\" | \"remote_agent\"</code></td><td>Task type of the registered background task, matching the <code>status</code> arm</td></tr><tr><td><code>workflowName</code></td><td><code>string</code></td><td>The <code>meta.name</code> from the workflow script</td></tr><tr><td><code>runId</code></td><td><code>string</code></td><td>Workflow run identifier to pass as <code>resumeFromRunId</code> on a later invocation. Absent for <code>remote_launched</code> runs, where the cloud session URL is the resume handle</td></tr><tr><td><code>summary</code></td><td><code>string</code></td><td>One-line description of what the workflow does</td></tr><tr><td><code>transcriptDir</code></td><td><code>string</code></td><td>Directory where subagent transcripts are written during execution</td></tr><tr><td><code>scriptPath</code></td><td><code>string</code></td><td>Path to the persisted workflow script for this run. the wake-up will fire as an epoch millisecond timestamp, the delay actually used, and whether the requested delay was clamped. The <code>stopped</code> field is <code>true</code> when the call ended the loop with <code>stop: true</code>. It requires Claude Code v2.1.202 or later. The <code>cancelledWakeups</code> field counts how many pending wakeups a <code>stop: true</code> call cancelled. A value of 0 means nothing was pending, and a recurring <code>/loop</code> cron isn’t cancelled by <code>stop: true</code>. local <code>path</code> that was published for the publish action, with <code>updated</code> set to true when the publish redeployed an existing artifact, and <code>warnings</code> carrying any publish-time advisories. The list action returns the <code>artifacts</code> rows instead, with <code>truncated</code> set when more artifacts exist than the requested limit. style=\"overflow:scroll\">\n<div style=\"min-width:fit-content\">\n<div>\n<table><thead><tr><th style=\"text-align:left\">Field</th><th style=\"text-align:left\">Type</th><th style=\"text-align:left\">Description</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>name</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>string</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\">The name the server is registered under, the same value <a href=\"#query-object\"><code>mcpServerStatus()</code></a> reports for it</td></tr><tr><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>source</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>string</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\">Where the server’s definition came from: <code>sdk</code>, <code>plugin</code>, or a configuration scope</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div> <span><code>source</code> takes one of the following values. The set is open, so treat a value you don’t recognize as a configured source, never as <code>sdk</code>:</span>\n<ul>\n<li><strong><code>sdk</code></strong>: an in-process server your application registered. Only the SDK host application can register one, so a configured server never reports <code>sdk</code>, whatever its name.</li>\n<li><strong><code>plugin</code></strong>: a server a <a href=\"/docs/en/agent-sdk/plugins\">plugin</a> provides. Its <code>name</code> is the scoped <code>plugin:<plugin-name>:<server-name></code> form described under <a href=\"/docs/en/mcp#plugin-provided-mcp-servers\">plugin-provided MCP servers</a>.</li>\n<li><strong>A configuration scope</strong>: <code>user</code>, <code>project</code>, <code>local</code>, <code>dynamic</code>, <code>managed</code>, <code>enterprise</code>, <code>claudeai</code>, or <code>agent</code>. A <code>.mcp.json</code> server reports <code>project</code>, and <a href=\"/docs/en/mcp#mcp-installation-scopes\">MCP installation scopes</a> defines <code>local</code>, <code>project</code>, and <code>user</code>. Servers your application passes in the <a href=\"#options\"><code>mcpServers</code> option</a>, other than in-process SDK servers, report <code>dynamic</code>.</li> </ul> <span>Base trust decisions on <code>source</code>, not on <code>name</code> or the <code>mcp__<server>__</code> tool-name prefix. For any source other than <code>sdk</code>, <code>name</code> is untrusted text: escape it before display.</span> <span><code>McpServerProvenance</code> and the fields that carry it require Agent SDK v0.3.274 or later.</span>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#mcpserverstatus\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span><code>McpServerStatus</code></span></h3> <span>Status of a connected MCP includes them, so don’t add the two together. The field is absent until a turn runs on a Claude Code version that records it, so a resumed session that began on an earlier version reports a partial count. <code>thinkingTokens</code> requires Agent SDK v0.3.257 or later.</span> <span>The <code>canonicalModel</code> and <code>provider</code> fields require Claude Code v2.1.218 or later. <code>canonicalModel</code> is the canonical model ID that the pricing lookup uses; it can differ from the raw model string that keys the entry, for example when that string is a provider-specific ID or an alias.</span> <span><code>provider</code> names the API backend that served the model, such as <code>firstParty</code>, <code>bedrock</code>, <code>vertex</code>, <code>foundry</code>, <code>anthropicAws</code>, <code>mantle</code>, or <code>gateway</code>.</span> <span><code>costBasis</code> names the price table that priced the model’s latest request: <code>list</code> for list price, <code>managed</code> for a <a href=\"/docs/en/settings-reference#modelpricing\"><code>modelPricing</code></a> table, or <code>unknown</code> when neither matched the model ID. The field requires Claude Code v2.1.246 or later.</span>\n<h3>\n<div><a <code>BetaOutputTokensDetails</code> are defined in <code>@anthropic-ai/sdk</code>.</span> <span><code>output_tokens_details</code> breaks the billed output down by category. It currently carries one field, <code>thinking_tokens: number</code>, counting the output tokens the model generated as internal reasoning, including the thinking-block delimiters. The <code>output_tokens_details</code> field requires TypeScript SDK v0.3.228 or later, which bundles Claude Code v2.1.228.</span>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Billing</strong>: read the breakdown for observability, not for billing. <code>output_tokens</code> stays the authoritative total, and <code>output_tokens - thinking_tokens</code> approximates the non-reasoning output.</li>\n<li><strong>What the count covers</strong>: the raw reasoning the model produced, which can be longer than the thinking text returned in the response body. The API computes it by re-tokenizing that raw text, so it can differ from the model’s exact generation count by a few tokens.</li>\n<li><strong>Streaming</strong>: on streamed assistant messages this breakdown, like <code>output_tokens</code>, is a <code>message_start</code> placeholder and carries no real count, so read it from the result message’s <code>usage</code> as <a href=\"/docs/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking#read-output-tokens-from-the-result-message\">Read output tokens from the result message</a> describes. On the result message, <code>thinking_tokens</code> reads <code>0</code> when the model or provider reports no breakdown.</li>\n<li><strong><code>null</code> cases</strong>: <code>output_tokens_details</code> itself is <code>null</code> on assistant messages Claude Code synthesizes, such as API-error messages.</li> </ul>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#calltoolresult\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span><code>CallToolResult</code></span></h3> <span>MCP tool result type (from <code>@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/types.js</code>). <code>structuredContent</code> is a JSON object that can be returned alongside <code>content</code>, including image blocks. Claude Code doesn’t send <code>display</code> to Amazon Bedrock or Google Cloud’s Agent Platform, so on those providers Opus 4.7 and later return empty <code>thinking</code> blocks even when you set style=\"--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E\"></div></div>\n<div>\n<div></div>\n<div><span>The <code>signal</code> field tells your spawn function when to tear down the process. Pass it as the <code>signal</code> option to Node’s <code>spawn()</code>, or pass it to your VM or container teardown handler.</span><span>This signal does not fire the instant <a href=\"#options\"><code>Options.abortController</code></a> aborts. The SDK first closes the process’s stdin and waits about two seconds so the CLI can shut down cleanly, then aborts this signal. To react the moment the caller aborts instead, listen on your own <code>Options.abortController.signal</code>, which your spawn function can reference from its enclosing scope.</span></div></div>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#mcpsetserversresult\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span><code>McpSetServersResult</code></span></h3> <span>Result of a <code>setMcpServers()</code> call <code>setMcpServers()</code>, Claude Code applies these rules:</span>\n<ul>\n<li><strong>Servers the call doesn’t name</strong>: Claude Code keeps plugin-provided servers running. Requires Agent SDK v0.3.210 or later.</li>\n<li><strong>Servers the call names</strong>: except for built-in servers the CLI started at startup, Claude Code replaces a running server only when its config differs from the one you passed.</li>\n<li><strong>Built-in servers the CLI started at startup</strong>: if the call names one, Claude Code drops that entry and reports it in <code>errors</code>.</li> </ul> <span>The promise resolves after newly added stdio, HTTP, and SSE servers connect or fail, so tools from servers that connected are available on the next turn.</span> <span><code>added</code> lists the servers Claude Code added or replaced, whether or not they connected. A server that failed to connect appears in both <code>added</code> and <code>errors</code>, with the failure text under <code>errors</code> and a <code>failed</code> row in <a href=\"#methods\"><code>mcpServerStatus()</code></a>. Before Claude Code v2.1.257, a server whose connection attempt threw was reported only under <code>errors</code>.</span>\n<h3>\n<div><a </code></pre></div></div></div></div>\n<div style=\"--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E\"></div></div> <span>When Claude Code <a href=\"/docs/en/mcp#automatic-backgrounding-of-long-tool-calls\">moves a long MCP tool call to the background</a>, the <code>tool_result</code> block for that call holds only a placeholder and the call’s real result arrives in this notification. Match the notification to the call with <code>tool_use_id</code>. On a <code>completed</code> notification, <code>resource_links</code> lists the files the tool returned by reference as <a href=\"#sdkmcpresourcelink\"><code>SDKMcpResourceLink</code></a> entries, with the same 50-link and 64 KiB limits as <a href=\"#sdkusermessage\"><code>tool_use_result.resourceLinks</code></a>. Claude Code omits <code>resource_links</code> when the result had no links and on notifications for tasks that aren’t MCP tool calls. <code>resource_links</code> requires Agent SDK v0.3.257 or later.</span> <span>Claude Code prepends a notice to every task notification it sends to the model, except deliveries stamped with the <a href=\"#task-notification-subkinds\"><code>scheduled-trigger</code> subkind</a>, which carry an assigned-task framing instead. The notice states that no human input has occurred, so the model doesn’t treat the notification as a user instruction or approval.</span> <span>To detect a task-notification turn, check <code>origin.kind === \"task-notification\"</code> on the <a href=\"#sdkusermessage\"><code>SDKUserMessage</code></a> or <a href=\"#sdkresultmessage\"><code>SDKResultMessage</code></a> rather than matching on the notice text. Read <code>subkind</code> from the same field if you need to know what raised it. Before v2.1.205, Claude Code left the notice off notifications that arrived while the session was idle.</span>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#sdkhookstartedmessage\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span><code>SDKHookStartedMessage</code></span></h3> <span>Emitted when a hook begins executing.</span> <span>Claude Code delivers this message, <a href=\"#sdkhookprogressmessage\"><code>SDKHookProgressMessage</code></a>, and <a href=\"#sdkhookresponsemessage\"><code>SDKHookResponseMessage</code></a> to the message stream immediately, style=\"--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E\"></div></div> <span>While a tool call runs in the main conversation, Claude Code emits a <code>tool_progress</code> message every 30 seconds with <code>heartbeat: true</code>. Each heartbeat carries the tool name and elapsed seconds, so you can distinguish a long-running call from a stalled session. Claude Code doesn’t emit heartbeats for tool calls inside a subagent. The <code>heartbeat</code> field requires Agent SDK v0.3.214 or later. Before v2.1.257, Claude Code didn’t emit heartbeats for a foreground Agent tool call either.</span> <span>On <code>tool_progress</code> messages for the Agent tool other than heartbeats, <code>subagent_type</code> names the running subagent type, such as <code>general-purpose</code>. <code>subagent_retry</code> is present while that subagent waits out an API error backoff, such as a rate limit or overload, with one message per retry attempt. Both fields require Agent SDK v0.3.214 or later.</span> <span>To render a retry indicator from <code>subagent_retry</code>:</span>\n<ul>\n<li>Track the indicator by <code>parent_tool_use_id</code>, which is unique per subagent. <code>tool_use_id</code> is shared by parallel subagents from one assistant turn, so tracking by it would let one subagent’s update clear another’s indicator.</li>\n<li>Clear the indicator when a later <code>tool_progress</code> for the same <code>parent_tool_use_id</code> arrives with neither <code>subagent_retry</code> nor <code>heartbeat: true</code>, or when the tool’s result message arrives. Frames with <code>heartbeat: true</code> report liveness only, so keep the indicator when one arrives. <code>attempt</code> can exceed <code>max_retries</code> under persistent retry, so don’t derive clearing from the counters.</li>\n<li>Treat <code>error_category</code> as a token for choosing your own message text, not as display text. The values are <code>rate_limit</code>, <code>overloaded</code>, <code>authentication_failed</code>, <code>server_error</code>, <code>cloud_credential_error</code>, and <code>unknown</code>. Handle a value you don’t recognize the way you handle <code>unknown</code>, because later releases can add values.</li> </ul>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#sdkauthstatusmessage\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span><code>SDKAuthStatusMessage</code></span></h3> <span>Emitted during authentication style=\"--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E\"></div></div> <span><code>ambient</code> is <code>true</code> for tasks that aren’t part of the session’s work, such as tasks Claude Code runs for its own operation. Live-update watchers are also ambient, including watchers the user asked for. Exclude ambient tasks from activity indicators. The field requires Agent SDK v0.3.247 or later.</span> <span><code>ambient</code> also appears on <a href=\"#sdktasknotificationmessage\"><code>SDKTaskNotificationMessage</code></a> and on <a href=\"#sdkbackgroundtaskschangedmessage\"><code>SDKBackgroundTasksChangedMessage</code></a> entries.</span> <span><code>is_backgrounded</code> and <code>spawn_depth</code> describe how Claude Code started the task. Both fields require Agent SDK v0.3.238 or later.</span>\n<ul>\n<li><code>is_backgrounded</code>: Claude Code sets it on <code>\"local_agent\"</code> and <code>\"local_bash\"</code> tasks. <code>true</code> means the task runs in the background. <code>false</code> means the task runs in the foreground, and the tool call that started it stays blocked until the task finishes or moves to the background.</li>\n<li><code>spawn_depth</code>: Claude Code sets it on <code>\"local_agent\"</code> tasks only. A subagent that the main thread spawned has depth <code>1</code>. A subagent that a depth <code>1</code> subagent spawned has depth <code>2</code>, and so on.</li> </ul> <span>A <a href=\"/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents#resume-subagents\">resumed subagent</a> always reports <code>is_backgrounded: true</code>, because Claude Code runs every resumed subagent in the background. When a foreground task moves to the background later, Claude Code reports the new <code>is_backgrounded</code> value in a <a href=\"#sdktaskupdatedmessage\"><code>task_updated</code></a> message rather than sending a second <code>task_started</code>.</span>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#sdktaskprogressmessage\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span><code>SDKTaskProgressMessage</code></span></h3> <span>Emitted periodically while a subagent or background task is running. style=\"--fade-color-light:#ffffff;--fade-color-dark:#0B0C0E\"></div></div>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#sdkbackgroundtaskschangedmessage\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span><code>SDKBackgroundTasksChangedMessage</code></span></h3> <span>Emitted whenever the set of live background tasks changes: a task starts, completes, is killed, a foreground agent is backgrounded, or a task’s <code>description</code> or <code>ambient</code> field changes.</span> <span>The <code>tasks</code> array is the full live set. Replace any cached set with each payload instead of pairing <code>task_started</code> and <code>task_notification</code> events, so the next membership change corrects any event you missed.</span> <span>Ordering relative to those per-task events is unspecified, so don’t correlate the two streams.</span> <span>Nothing is emitted at startup. Reset to an empty set whenever the session’s CLI process starts or restarts and let the next membership change repopulate it.</span> <span>When you send a repeated <code>initialize</code> control request to a running session, such as with <a href=\"#query-object\"><code>reinitialize()</code></a> after a transport gap, Claude Code follows the response with a snapshot of the current live set, even when it is empty. A reconnecting host therefore learns what is running without waiting for the next membership change. Before Agent SDK v0.3.239, Claude Code sent no snapshot after a repeated <code>initialize</code>.</span> <span>Requires Claude Code v2.1.203 or rejection is from a claude.ai subscription whose included usage is exhausted, and the session cannot continue until the user buys usage credits. <code>canUserPurchaseCredits</code> indicates whether the authenticated user can buy credits for the account, and <code>hasChargeableSavedPaymentMethod</code> indicates whether a saved payment method is on file. All three fields are absent on rate-limit events that are not credits-required rejections. The <code>commands</code> array is the full updated list, so replace any cached command list with this payload. In earlier SDK versions, <code>supportedCommands()</code> returns the snapshot captured at initialization and never reflects mid-session args?: string[] }</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>undefined</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\">Custom ripgrep binary configuration for sandbox environments</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>\n<div>\n<div></div>\n<div><span>The sandbox depends on platform support and, on Linux, tools like <code>bubblewrap</code> and <code>socat</code>. When <code>enabled</code> is <code>true</code> and the sandbox can’t start, <code>query()</code> reports a <code>result</code> message with <code>subtype: \"error_during_execution\"</code> and the reason in <code>errors</code>. For a single message <code>query()</code> call, the SDK throws after yielding that error result, so wrap the loop in a try block to continue past it. See <a href=\"/docs/en/agent-sdk/agent-loop#handle-the-result\">Handle the result</a> for the error contract.</span><span>To run unsandboxed instead, set <code>failIfUnavailable: <code>allowUnixSockets</code> option can grant access to system services that reach outside the sandbox. For example, allowing <code>/var/run/docker.sock</code> effectively grants full host system access through the Docker API, bypassing sandbox isolation. Only allow Unix sockets that are strictly necessary and understand the security implications of each.</span></div></div>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#sandboxnetworkconfig\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span><code>SandboxNetworkConfig</code></span></h3> <span>Network-specific configuration for sandbox mode. names that sandboxed processes can access</td></tr><tr><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>deniedDomains</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>string[]</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>[]</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\">Domain names that sandboxed processes cannot access. Requires Claude Code v2.1.219 or later</td></tr><tr><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>allowManagedDomainsOnly</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>boolean</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>false</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\">Managed-settings only. When set in <a href=\"/docs/en/managed-settings\">managed settings</a>, only <code>allowedDomains</code> entries and <code>WebFetch(domain:...)</code> allow rules from managed settings are honored, and allow entries from user, project, or local settings are ignored. Has no effect when set via SDK options</td></tr><tr><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>allowLocalBinding</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>boolean</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>false</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\">Allow processes to bind to local ports (e.g., for dev servers)</td></tr><tr><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>allowUnixSockets</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>string[]</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>[]</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\">Unix socket paths that processes can access (e.g., Docker socket)</td></tr><tr><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>allowAllUnixSockets</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>boolean</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>false</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\">Allow access to all Unix sockets</td></tr><tr><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>httpProxyPort</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>number</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>undefined</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\">HTTP proxy port for network requests</td></tr><tr><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>socksProxyPort</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>number</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\"><code>undefined</code></td><td style=\"text-align:left\">SOCKS proxy port for network requests</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>\n<div>\n<div></div>\n<div><span>The built-in sandbox proxy enforces <code>allowedDomains</code> based on the requested hostname and does not terminate or inspect TLS traffic, so techniques such as <a href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domain_fronting\">domain fronting</a> can potentially bypass it. See <a href=\"/docs/en/sandboxing#security-limitations\">Sandboxing security limitations</a> for details and <a href=\"/docs/en/agent-sdk/secure-deployment#traffic-forwarding\">Secure deployment</a> for configuring a TLS-terminating proxy.</span></div></div>\n<h3>\n<div><a href=\"#sandboxfilesystemconfig\">​\n<div></div></a></div><span><code>SandboxFilesystemConfig</code></span></h3> Commands</span></h3> <span>When <code>allowUnsandboxedCommands</code> is enabled, the model can request to run commands outside the sandbox by setting <code>dangerouslyDisableSandbox: true</code> in the tool input. These requests fall back to the existing permissions system, meaning your <code>canUseTool</code> handler is invoked, allowing you to implement custom authorization logic. Commands listed in <code>excludedCommands</code> instead bypass the sandbox automatically, with no model involvement; see <a href=\"#sandboxsettings\"><code>SandboxSettings</code></a>.</span> <span>In the example below, <code>isCommandAuthorized</code> stands in for an authorization check you define.</span>\n<div>\n<div>\n<div><button></button></div>\n<div><button></button><span></span></div>\n<div><button></button></div></div>\n<div
